Executive Summary
i

Public procurement or government procurement is defined as “an official procedure for
acquiring goods and services, including facility construction services and public projects,
througgovernment agencies.”
Azerbaijan, located at a strategic point along the coast of the Caspian Sea, is the center of
science and technology in the Eurasian region and has great importance for cooperation in
the international energy market as a production and transit point for crude oil and natural
gas. Azerbaijan is hugely reliant on the oil and gas industry, which accounts for more than
90% of its total exports, and has an economic structure trade that is contingent upon three
neighbouring countries (Russia, Iran, and Turkey). In other words, it is a country that is
economically and socially affected by unstable fluctuations in international oil prices and by
changes in the circumstances of neighbouring countries.
Recently the Azerbaijan government resolved to move away from excessive dependence on
the oil industry and make the transition to a knowledge-based economy, in a bid to avoid a
financial crisis caused by unstable oil prices and to create a democratic political environment.
It is in this context that the government established 'Azerbaijan 2020: Look into the Future’
and 'Azerbaijan 2030: National Priorities for Socio-Economic Development' with the aim of
achieving sustainable economic development based on innovation in the field of science
and technology. According to the Azerbaijan National Cooperation Strategy (2016), it is
shown that the government is making efforts to strengthen the national development plan-
related science and technology innovation capacity and achieve industrial diversification
policies in order to build national competitiveness. In addition, it emphasizes the need to
increase investment in science and technology innovation and to improve the operation of
its system by increasing the efficiency of knowledge creation, technology transfer, and the
application and commercialization of technology through the 'Azerbaijan 2030 Strategic
Roadmap'.
The High Technologies Park (HTP) was established in order to ensure the development of
science in accordance with modern requirements, to form an innovative economic

Executive Summary

===== PDF_PAGE 4 =====
2022 K-Innovation Partnership Program with Azerbaijan
ii
ecosystem in the country and the region, to use scientific and research potential effectively,
and to support business entrepreneurship. In accordance with its mission, the HTP aims to
provide joint activities with leading local and international organizations and scientific
institutions of various countries that can contribute to business entrepreneurship,
innovative research and development, and the development of the knowledge economy in
the country.
Since 2018 STEPI has supported the HTP in its efforts to develop its institutional capacity
through the K-Innovation Partnership Program. This program plays an important role in the
transfer and commercialization of technology and the incubation of start-ups, as well as
other strategic activities of the HTP. This cooperation between the two organizations aims
to commercialize the results of scientific research and promote their application, determine
the role of academia-industry cooperation in the development of existing entrepreneurial
activities, and foster a business environment that is conducive to adapting the scientific
research works of the HTP and other scientific research institutions to the requirements of
industry and real business, including the agricultural sector.
The 2022 project aims to provide policy consultation on improving the national system of
technology transfer and commercialization in Azerbaijan and capacity building of the HTP.
Moreover, it is intended to promote mutually beneficial economic cooperation between the
two countries by contributing to the development of science and technology and improving
the technology commercialization ecosystem in Azerbaijan.
With the activity of the project for the year 2022, the following expectations have been
defined:
• Capacity building of the stakeholders of Azerbaijan’s innovation ecosystem.
• Contribution to the formation of human capital in Azerbaijan.
• Expansion of Azerbaijan -Korea scientific, technology , and innovation (STI)
cooperation.
• Improvement of the operation program of the HTP.
• Further improvement of the process of technology commercialization observed
with the transformation of knowledge into economic value.

===== PDF_PAGE 5 =====
Executive Summary
iii
• Formation of a new entrepreneurial environment based on South Korea’s
experience of start-up and business incubation.

In the 1st year of the project, the goal is to increase Azerbaijan’s capacity for technology
transfer and commercialization services and, in the 2nd year, its capacity for start-up and
incubation services. These include the transfer of knowledge, knowledge laboratories, and
seminars, and the promotion of Korea-Azerbaijan cooperation projects, etc. In the 3rd year
of the project, it is planned to implement the implementation process in Azerbaijan by
strengthening its capacity in the fields of technology transfer and commercialization, start-
up, and incubation. The project will offer services for technology transfer -
commercialization and start-up - incubation operational programs as a continuation of the
strategic plan and the road map developed as a result of the successful operation of STEPI
with the HTP. We hope that this K-Innovation program will make a valuable contribution to
fostering firm foundations for technology commercialization in Azerbaijan.

.

Chapter 1. Project Overview
3

1. Project Background
Azerbaijan, located at a strategic point along the coast of the Caspian Sea, is the centre of
science and technology in the Eurasian region and has great importance for cooperation in
the international energy market as a production and transit point for crude oil and natural
gas. Azerbaijan is hugely reliant on the oil and gas industry, which accounts for more than
90% of its total exports, and has an economic structure trade that is contingent upon three
neighbouring countries (Russia, Iran, and Turkey). In other words, it is a country that is
economically and socially affected by unstable fluctuations in international oil prices and by
changes in the circumstances of neighbouring countries.

Recently the Azerbaijan government resolved to move away from excessive dependence on
the oil industry and make the transition to a knowledge-based economy, in a bid to avoid a
financial crisis caused by unstable oil prices and to create a democratic political environment.
It is in this context that the government established 'Azerbaijan 2020: Look into the Future’
and 'Azerbaijan 2030: National Priorities for Socio-Economic Development' with the aim of
achieving sustainable economic development based on innovation in the field of science
and technology. According to the Azerbaijan National Cooperation Strategy (2016), it is
shown that the government is making efforts to strengthen the national development plan-
related science and technology innovation (STI) capacity and achieve industrial
diversification policies in order to build national competitiveness. In addition, it emphasizes
the need to increase investment in science and technology innovation and to improve the
operation of its system by increasing t he efficiency of knowledge creation, technology
transfer, and the application and commercialization of technology through the 'Azerbaijan
2030 Strategic Roadmap'.

===== PDF_PAGE 17 =====
Chapter 1. Project Overview
5
The Science and Technology Policy Institute (STEPI) received a request for continuous
cooperation from the National Academy of Sciences (ANAS), an institution dedicated to
implementing Azerbaijan's national science and technology innovation policy.
As a result, STEPI implemented two projects, namely 'STI Policy Development and
Management & Operation of STI Parks in Azerbaijan' in 2018 and 'Promotion of Technology
Transfer and Commercialization in Azerbaijan' in 2019 -2020, under the K-Innovation
Partnership Program.
According to the information collected during the execution of these projects in 2018-2020,
Azerbaijan expects to acquire South Korea's ample experience and know-how of practical
science and technology innovation policies including technology transfer and
commercialization, STI park management and operation, STI human resource development,
national R&D program establishment, technology management and STI strategies. More
specifically, the requested agenda includes improving the national technology transfer and
commercialization system and developing technology transfer and programs for the High
Technologies Park (HTP).
Thus, 2022 project had anticipated to contribute to fulfil the demands of HTP and strategic
partnership through knowledge sharing and policy consultation from Korean experiences.
Due to the COVID-19 pandemic, capacity-building programs, including invitational training
in Korea and other workshops and seminars, were carried out online, and current status
surveys were conducted in writing by local researchers.

===== PDF_PAGE 18 =====
2022 K-Innovation Partnership Program with Azerbaijan
6

2. Project Objectives
The 2022 project aims to provide policy consultation on improving the national system of
technology transfer and commercialization in Azerbaijan and capacity-building for
developing operational programs of the HTP. Furthermore, it is intended to promote
mutually beneficial economic cooperation between the two countries by contributing to the
development of STI in Azerbaijan according to the 『FRAMEWORK ACT ON INTERNATIONAL
DEVELOPMENT COOPERATION』.
[Table 1-2] Framework Act on International Development Cooperation
Article 3. Basic Ideas and Objectives
(1) The basic objectives of international development cooperation are to reduce poverty in
developing nations, improve the human rights of women, children and people with
disabilities, achieve gender equality, realize sustainable development and
humanitarianism, promote mutually beneficial economic relationships with cooperation
partners, and pursue peace and prosperity in the international community.
(2) The objectives of international development cooperation shall lie in achieving the
following matters to pursue the basic ideas presented under paragraph (1):
The Basic Ideas of
International
Development
Cooperation
Development of developing nations and improvement of the system and
conditions for such development.
Promotion of friendly relationships and mutual exchanges with developing
nations.
Contribution to the resolution of global problems related to international
development cooperation.
Source: Framework Act on International Development Cooperation, No.17302(2020), Republic of Korea.

• Project Objectives: 1st year (2022) - 3rd year (2024)
- (2022) Improvement of the TC System in Azerbaijan and Development of the TC
Program at ANAS-HTP.
- (2023) Improvement of the Start-up and Incubation System in Azerbaijan and
Development of the Start-up and Incubation Program at HTP.
- (2024) Implementation of the Application Process with the Implementation of
Capacity Building of the TC Start-up and Incubation System in Azerbaijan.

===== PDF_PAGE 19 =====
Chapter 1. Project Overview
7

3. Project Framework
The fundamental concept of this project is to strengthen the capabilities of Azerbaijan’s
experts by sharing Korea’s experience and through consultation. It aims to encourage them
to develop the 'Technology Commercialization System Improvement Plan' and the 'HTP
Operation Program' by themselves. Diverse approaches were taken to building their
capacity and achieving outcomes.
First of all, a research team composed of local experts was formed to investigate the current
status of technology commercialization systems and programs in Azerbaijan. The aim was
to enable them to determine by themselves what needed to be improved and why before
deriving strategic plans. Based on this study, the Korean consultants diagnosed the
problems and challenges they were facing and identified the key points for policy
recommendations.
Secondly, the Technology Commercialization Knowledge Lab (TCK-Lab) and the Training
Workshop were conducted to share Korea’s experiences and methodologies. The TCK-Lab
is a regular activity conducted in the form of a learning lab consisting of lectures and
discussions by Korean experts in each specialized field related to technology transfer and
commercialization. The t raining workshop was a one -off event that included a more
comprehensive range of subjects. Both activities were opened in an on-offline hybrid.
Thirdly, the Technology Transfer and Commercialization Platform (TTCP) was developed to
activate the technology commercialization ecosystem. The experts from both countries
have jointly provided an advisory on this platform design. TTCP is expected to function
which enables of archiving various sources and information, as well as collaboration and
networking between all stakeholder in industry-university-research institutes.

1. Capacity Building

 Technology Commercialization Knowledge Lab (TCK-Lab)
The Technology Commercialization Knowledge Lab (TCK-Lab), which was held from March
to September 2022, consisted of seven specialized topics in the field of technology
commercialization. Korean experts delivered the lectures focusing on theory and Korean
cases. In addition, the lectures emphasized the importance of governance and networking
in establishing an ecosystem for technology commercialization, as well as methodologies
for preparing the legal grounds and activation. Azerbaijan’s experts explored strategies that
could be applied to Azerbaijan’s circumstances based on Korean cases, and engaged in-
depth discussions based on a Q&A with Korean experts. To gather the results of the TCK-
Labs, a survey on satisfaction has been conducted, in which the participants suggested the
following three improvement factors: (1) needs for detailed scenario for Azerbaijan; (2)
additional time for Q&A ; and (3) a n arrower range of topics and contents. The total
cumulative number of learners in the 2022 TCK-Lab was 92, exceeding the initial target
number of 50, ultimately achieving a performance score of 184%.

 Online Training Workshop
The invitational training program was replaced with an online training workshop due to new
restrictions on immigration between the two countries due to the continuous spread of
COVID-19. This workshop included practical lectures with a focus on cases of technology
commercialization cooperation between industry, academia, and research institutes.
Furthermore, an expert review session was held on the first draft of the strategic plan for
the technology commercialization system and the development of the HTP program, which
are the final outputs of the 2022 project.

2. Policy Consultation

 Technology Transfer & Commercialization Platform (TTCP)
In order to activate the technology commercialization ecosystem in Azerbaijan, a platform
is needed in which information and data can be easily accessed by diverse stakeholders
including researchers, policymakers, technology developers, start-ups, SMEs, enterprises,
research institutes, and Techno Parks. The main function of such a platform would be to
provide solutions designed to address the needs of stakeholders. Moreover, networking
and partnerships between diverse sectors could also be achieved by uploading the profiles
of individuals and organizations on to this platform.
The process of platform development over the past six months is as follows:
• (May) Establishment of the basic plan for the platform: STEPI project team,
Azerbaijan experts , and IT company 'CODERS' organized regular meetings to
determine the contents and development plan
• (June-September) Starting with the UI/UX design, front-end and back -end
development were carried out. Notably, the interface is design ed to be easy for
anyone to use
• (October) The open beta version service opened , and it has been collecting user
feedback from technology commercialization officials to improve technical and
qualitative issues
• (November-December) Based on the feedback collected by November, the
upgraded version will be released in December with corrections and supplements

Sustainable management, including server maintenance and the construction of a database,
was required, so collaboration with local ICT companies was essential. As a platfor m
developer, the local company CODERS had a high level of understanding of the project and
the technology commercialization field because it operates start-up acceleration programs
and boot camps. Currently, the primary form of the platform has been completed, and the
open beta service is in operation. Preparation of legal registration for the formalization of
the platform, as well as app development, will proceed in 2023.

 Sharing the Knowledge Acquired from Korea’s Experiences
Before sharing Korea's experience and know-how and success cases, it was necessary to
review the results of a study on the current status of the technology commercialization
system in Azerbaijan. In addition, to activate the technology commercialization ecosystem,
it was necessary to examine the existing conditions, including the limitations and challenges..
The case studies of Korea’s experiences were largely divided into the following three themes:
Governance of Technology Commercialization, Intellectual Property Rights System, and
Start-up Support Programs.
First, an overview of the technology commercialization system, the legal basis and
significant policy were introduced, and the main projects of each department of the central
government were classified in detail . Moreover, the support policy of the central
government and performance compared to R&D investment in Azerbaijan were analysed
to reveal any deficiencies and derive the implications.
Second, the history and process of development in establishing a virtuous cycle of the
intellectual property ecosystem in Korea were shared. Regarding policy consultation on the
systematic development of the intellectual property system. The history of development of
the intellectual property system was introduced, and the importance of patent rights issues
and inventions was emphasized.
Lastly, noteworthy start-up support programs in Korea were introduced for the
development of the HTP's operating programs. As a specific case, information on Korean I-
Corp, a start-up support program through the commercialization of science and technology,
was shared. While accentuating the need for technology start-up education and training,
practical implications were suggested for preparing HTP's start-up support program, based
on Korean I-Corp experience, as a market-linked start-up exploration support project based
on public technology.

===== PDF_PAGE 39 =====
Chapter 2. Project Performance
27
 Consultation on Strategic Plans
The ‘Strategic Plans’, one of the main outputs of the 2022 project, were drawn up by the
Azerbaijani experts based on a study of the current situation and the advice of Korean
consultants. The strategic plans were carried out in the following phases under the following
two themes: 1) Improvement of the technology commercialization system at the national
level; and 2) Development of the HTP operational program at the institutional level.
① First, the current system’s strengths, weaknesses, and problems were analysed based
on a study on the current status of Azerbaijan’s experts.
② Then, Korean consultants diagnosed the circumstances and conditions based on the
results of that study.
③ Furthermore, Korea’s methodologies, approaches, and experiences were shared in
the form of various capacity-building activities, such as TCK -Labs and an online
training workshop, to enable Azerbaijan’s experts to devise strategic plans by
themselves.
④ Consequently, the final strategic plans were established based on the previous status
diagnosis, the sharing of Korea’s knowledge and experiences, policy
recommendations, and capacity-building activities.

1. Technology Commercialization

 Introduction
The rapid development of technology and increasing competition are among the main
factors that justify the commercialization of science, along with the actualization of
innovative entrepreneurship. Gaining added value through technology transfer or the
adoption of an innovative approach plays a key role in competition and development. One
can observe, through the experience of developed societies , that the development of
innovations and their application in real life are based on extensive, long -term and
necessary resources, as well as being more productive with multidisciplinary approaches. In
this process, the commercialization of science and technology transfer activities are
considered to be the most important factor in building competitiveness , while the
competitive advantages of innovation are becoming increasingly important for investors.
Examples confirming these views can be seen in the market value of brands f ormed in
accordance with the requirements of the information society. For example, all the activities
of Apple and Amazon, which now have a collective market value of $ 1 trillion (US), are
based on knowledge, in other words, the application of new and high technologies. These
and other examples show that well-designed educational and research processes that lead
to high-tech production are the most important factors in making today’s societies stronger.

 Technology Policy in Economic Development
Technology policies are generally aimed at studying and monitoring technological changes
and technological developments around the world, as well as increasing the economic
Chapter 3. Current Status of the
Technology Commercialization
System and Programs in Azerbaijan

===== PDF_PAGE 44 =====
2022 K-Innovation Partnership Program with Azerbaijan
32
importance of scientific research (R&D). In other words, technology policies determine the
forms of state intervention in the economy in knowledge-based approaches by influencing
(or playing a role in) the process by which technological change takes place.1 At present,
countries that have more accurately identified the needs of the current era are pursuing
medium- and long-term technology policies. As such, technology policy can be seen as a
part of industrial policy. Increasing productivity through the application of technological
innovations also affects technology policy, which is considered the most important factor in
the industrial policy of many countries. Taking this into account, one can say that a country's
industrial policy should be reorganized within the framework of the requirements arising
from production capacity and t echnology transfer, based on the development and
application of technological innovations.2
In a free market, the existence of and need for technological innovation are determined by
the market participants. However, it should be noted that the state interferes in this process
for a number of reasons. The creation of legal regulatory mechanisms necessary for the
conditions of competition and organization of the competitive environment is enabled with
the direct support of the state. In order to ensure a healthy development process and make
it work fairly, it is necessary to guarantee products and services that have been developed
on the basis of technological innovation in accordance with the law. Such legal regulations
cover not only physical products, but also, in some cases, unrealized ideas. As a natural
consequence of this situation, monopolies are inevitable in some cases. Potentially, in the
defense sector and certain other areas, the fact that only the state is a customer, it has a
long-term say in this area, as well as the need to further regulate this policy. In general,
technology can be obtained in two ways: technology transfer and technology production.
For a country or enterprise, the transfer method can only be considered in the short term,
because while transfer is profitable in the short term. This is because continuous updates
are required according to the technology cycle. In fact, it is possible to end up as a
"technology consumer" rather than a "technology producer" through the transfer of
technology. Meanwhile, technology production is an initiative that does not produce

1 Erol Taymaz, “Sanayi ve Teknoloji Politikaları: Amaçlar ve Araçlar”, ODTÜ Gelişme Dergisi, 20(4), 1993, s. 550.
2 İsa Qasımov, Fərid Cəbiyev, “İnnivasiya sahibkarlığı və texnoparklar”, 2017, Bakı, s. 24.

Chapter 3. Current Status of the Technology Commercialization System and Programs in Azerbaijan
33
immediate results in the short term, but can benefit a country in the long run. Technology
production requires patience, time, and important scientific research. For example, it
sometimes takes ten years or more for a technology park to produce the expected result or
for a given investment to pay off. Although the policy of acquiring technology through
production is a difficult and long-term process, it is more profitable than technology transfer.
In Azerbaijan there are complex infrastructure factors that regulate and ensure the process
of technology transfer and commercialization. It should be noted that the work related to
the transfer and commercialization of technology in this country is in the process of forming
an ecosystem. While in some cases the infrastructure opportunities created in this direction
are sufficient, there are problems in ensuring efficiency in the use of such infrastructure.
This report provides information on existing infra structure opportunities and their
administrative management. The government is striving to ensure balanced and sustainable
economic development in the country, improve the social welfare of the population, and
create a qualitatively new model of development by creating organic interconnection and
coordination between current, medium and long -term periods of socio -economic
development, as well as accelerating progress in all areas. In this regard, in accordance with
the Order of the President of the Republic of Azerbaijan No. 1862, dated November 29, 2011,
the development concept titled "Azerbaijan 2020: Vision for the Future" has been
developed with the participation of the relevant government agencies, scientific
organizations and highly qualified specialists, and through effective cooperation with
international organizations.3 Support for scientific potential and innovation activities has a
special place in the Development Concept. In accordance with the requirements of the
Development Concept, it should be noted that a lot of work has been done in Azerbaijan to
support scientific potential and innovation, especially in the field of infrastructure.
Paragraph 4.3 of the Concept for supporting scientific potential and innovation prescribes
the following: One of the main directions will be the expansion of innovation activities to
ensure long-term sustainable economic development, "to form a knowledge economy,

accelerate the creation of science-based technology and products (works, services). In this
regard, the development of science will be prioritized and progressive, and the process of
its effective integration into global science will be continued. The scientific infrastructure
will be improved, the material and technical base of science will be modernized, and the
transfer of information support systems in this area to electronic form will be ensured.4
Paragraph 3.2 of the “Azerbaijan 2030: National Priorities for Socio -Economic
Development”, which was approved by the Decree of the President of the Republic of
Azerbaijan dated February 2, 2021 (№ 2469) under the heading “Creative and Innovative
Society”, provides the following:5
"In the future, the digital society, which supports the latest technologies and innovations,
will become a leading force of development. An ecosystem that stimulates creativity and
innovation in society must be built to keep the country competitive. The ecosystem to be
established in the new period (relevant incentives, incubation and acceleration centers,
public-private partnership financing models, venture and crowdfunding institutions, etc.)
should be aimed at increasing the technological capacity of the economy. Prospects must
be created for Azerbaijan to become a space for technological exports by adapting to the
rapid technological progress currently taking place all around the world. To this end, the
development of the processing industry should be deepened, and the development of
science-intensive and high-income medium and high-tech industries should be ensured.
Furthermore, competitive funding mechanisms should be established to promote results-
oriented research.”
In general, a lot of work has been done to achieve high technological development, both in
terms of organization and the formation of infrastructure s, based on the existing legal
framework and the requirements of state programs. In the activities of individual ministries
and other law enforcement agencies, studies of the new challenges facing the world were
taken into account and the relevant commitments were made. Such approaches have laid

Social-Economic Development Strategy of the Republic of Azerbaijan, 2022-2026 6
The 2022-2026 Strategy adopted on July 22, 2022 underlines the essential points regarding
the development of innovations and technologi es. As the Strategy highlights, since
Azerbaijan is entering a new period of strategic development in the post-pandemic and
post-conflict era, its strategic aims include, but are not limited to, the following:
• Mobilization of modern and sustainable sources of economic growth through
structural and institutional reforms;
• Strengthening the innovation-orientation and financial sustainability of the national
economy; and
• Effective integration of the national economy into global value chains, thereby
• Achieving new targets in building a high-wealth society.

In order to achieve these aims, Azerbaijan will foster resources and provide services in order
to boost exports, innovations, and private-public partnerships, etc. along with other
strategic tools. One of the elements of the 2022-2026 Strategy that attracts attention is the
government’s intention to promote innovations and expand the implementation of modern
technological solutions, and thereby ensure economic development based on effectiveness
and competitiveness.
The 2022-2026 Strategy expects a number of important global trends, challenges and
opportunities to arise in the next decade, including the following:
• Expansion of efforts to implement the outcomes of intellectual activities in major
economic areas, and provision of support for innovation and creativity . (For this
reason, the development of a creative economy dominated by intellectual property
should be encouraged and supported.)

• Ideological basis for the projected deep structural reforms in the new social -

productive investments, intellectual properties that boost innovation, and
competitive markets are at the core of this ideology.)

One of the five strategic frameworks of the 2022 -2026 strategy concerns “competitive
human capital and the destination of modern innovations”. This framework sets, but is not
limited to, the following goals:
• Research in various fields of science will be expanded (attraction of modern-minded
and creative youth to scientific research in the early stages; establis hment of a
mechanism for competitive and purposeful funding of scientific research; increas e
in the number of published articles in impact-factored journals by 40 percent).
• Commercialization of scientific research, technical -construction projects (in other
words, R&D projects) will be supp orted (financing such research ; developing such
infrastructure; since commercializing technologies are a priority in innovative
development) by providing an environment for transforming research findings into
a product and eliminating obstacles to scientific research; thereby easing the
transfer of innovations to the markets and increasing R&D expenditure to 0.1% of
GDP.
• For the purpose s of import -substitution and the localization of production, local
production potential will be strengthened ( by incentivizing the implementation of
innovations in the private and public sectors , and promoting private sector
investments in IT infrastructure, resulting in an increase in the R&D expenditures of
transnational companies in the country).
• Quality and accessibility of education in innovative and digital skills will be expanded
(by preparing professional leaders for talent development and re -education via a
newly founded digital academy, and establishing cooperation with top international
technology universities in IT education, thereby increasing the number of graduates
of technical, technological and innovation-oriented study programs every year).
• A funding mechanism for all stages of start-up growth will be established ( by
developing a system of purposeful fiscal incentives for start-ups and investors, and

providing a sufficient pool of talents, resulting in an increase in the number of start-

• The “Absheron Valley” Innovation Cluster will be established ( by forming an

in priority fields of the economy, resulting in an increase in the number of residents
of companies moving into the cluster every year).

The Action Plan of the 2022 -2026 Strategy lists the tasks before which the relevant
authorities need to achieve in the coming years. Some of the relevant tasks included within
the scope of this report are as follows:
• Goal 1.1. Sustainable and high economic growth
- Increasing the effectiveness, targets and transparency of mechanisms to support
entrepreneurs;
- Expanding opportunities for SMEs to access resources (including financial
resources up to 30 million manats for project funding) and capacity-building and
clustering, etc.
- Diversifying and increasing the volume of exports of non -oil goods and services
(promoting the “Made in Azerbaijan” brand, etc.) and incentivizing FDI in non-oil
industries (i.e. attracting at least six companies listed on the Fortune 500 and
Fortune 2000 indices, etc.).
- Speeding up the transition to the digital economy (by adopting and promoting a
strategy for the development of the digital economy, increasing implementation
of Industry 4.0 technologies by 5% every year, etc.).
- Establishing industrial zones and increasing the potential of economic zones.
- Supporting the dynamic growth of the culture and creative industries and their
export potential (increasing their share if GDP by 10% every year, their exports
by 10% every year, etc.).
- Improving competition in the economy and strengthening the competitiveness
of entrepreneurship (by adopting the draft Competition Code, etc.).

• Goal 3.1. Education in accordance with the requirements of the 21st century

① Azerbaijan National Academy of Sciences (ANAS)7
By decree of the Council of the People’s Commissars of the USSR dated January 23, 1945,
the Azerbaijan Affiliate Branch was transformed into the Azerbaijan Academy of Sciences.
At that time the Academy comprised four sections, sixteen Scientific Research Institutes and
Scientific Research Departments, three museums, the Central Scientific Library, and various
scientific bases in Nakhchivan, Ganja, Khankendi and Guba. Within the same year. Thus,
after more than two months of checks and discussions beginning on January 23, 1945, the
first fifteen founders of the Academy of Sciences of the Azerb aijan SSR, and the first
academicians, were determined appointed. Under the decree of the Council of People's
Commissars of the Azerbaijan SSR on “The establishment of the Academy of Sciences of the
Azerbaijan SSR" dated March 27, 1945, No. 316, the establishment of the Academy of
Sciences of Azerbaijan was formalized and its Charter approved.
ANAS consists of the following six departments: Department of Physical-Mathematical and
Technical Sciences (Institutes of Radiation Problems, Physics, Mathematics and Mechanics,
Information Technology, Control Systems and Shamakhi Astrophysical Observatory, named
after Nasraddin Tusi, Institute of Biophysics); Department of Chemical Sciences (İnstitute of
Petrochemical Processes, named after the academician Yusif Mammadaliyev, İnstitute of
Catalysis and İnorganic Chemistry , named after the a cademician Murtuza Naghiyev,
İnstitute of Chemistry of Additives, named after the academician Ali Guliyev, İnstitute of
Polymer Materials); Department of Biological and Medical Sciences (Institute of Botany,
Institute of Molecular Biology and Biotechnologies, Institute of Zoology, Institute of
Physiology, named after the a cademician Abdulla Garayev, Institute of Microbiology,
Institute of Dendrology, Central Botanical Garden, Institute of Genetic Resources, Institute
of Soilscience and Agrochemistry) ; Department of Humanitarian Sciences (Institute of
Literature, named after Nizami Ganjavi, İnstitute of Linguistics, named after Nasimi, İnstitute
of Folklore, İnstitute of Architecture and Art, National Museum of Azerbaijan Literature,
named after Nizami Ganjavi, Huseyn Javid's Memorial Flat, Institute of Manuscripts, named
after Mahammad Fuzuli); Department of Social Sciences (Institute of History, named after

② Science Development Fund under the President of the Republic of Azerbaijan8
The Science Development Fund was established under the President of the Republic of
Azerbaijan, by Decree No. 526 of the President of the Republic of Azerbaijan dated October
21, 2009, in order to stimulate fundamental scientific research in the Republic of Azerbaijan,
strengthen research and studies on the country’s natural resources, and its cultural and
historical heritages, and ensure the proper representation of Azerbaijan’s scientific efforts

8 https://www.sdf.gov.az/en//generic/menu/Detail/101/menu/, 06.02.2022.

===== PDF_PAGE 54 =====
2022 K-Innovation Partnership Program with Azerbaijan
42
and achievements in the global scientific arena.
The main purpose of the Fund is to preserve and maintain the country’s scientific-technical
potential, to effectively use this potential for the development of the national economy, and
to enhance the role of science and techn ology in solving important social problems, as
introduced by the scientific organizations of the Republic of Azerbaijan, scientific institutes,
and other appropriate organizations and pe rsons working in natural, technical,
humanitarian and social fields, bearing importance for the state and society, fundamental,
practical and research-innovation scientific-research, programs, projects and other scientific
events, and to stimulate the scientific activities of the country’s scientists and create
additional opportunities.
The other important goals of the Science Development Fund are to enlarge the scientific
information and innovation resources of the state, promote the exploration and
investigation of the country’s natural resources and its cultural and historical heritages,
stimulate the productivity of research, support the representation of Azerbaijan in the
global scientific arena, deepen the relations hips between foreign and international
organizations that operate in the science-research field, learn and implement foreign
practices, build collaboration with non-state and other organizations, exchange scientific
information, and obtain mass character of the science.
Starting from the first of day of its operation, the Science Development Fund set out to
actively support the development of science, disseminate information on the scientific
results achieved in Azerbaijan to other countries , renovate or strengthen on a very
contemporary level the scientific researches logistical devices and gadgets based in scientific
organizations, and, at the same time, place a special emphasis on fostering young cadres,
organizing different scientific events, enabling scientists to acquire experience abroad, and
maintaining scientific editions and the solutions or other issues like that as a goal. In this
way, the Foundation serves to provide an additional and effective stimulus, agility, and new
approaches, and also paves the way for collaboration between young scientists working
within academic and educational systems and other organizations in the country.

③ Supreme Attestation Commission under the President of the Republic of
Azerbaijan
The Higher Attestation Commission was established by the Decree of the President of the
Republic of Azerbaijan dated April 23, 1992 under the Cabinet of Ministers of the Republic
of Azerbaijan, and was later subordinated to the State Committee for Science and
Technology of the Republic of Azerbaijan. By the Decree of the President of the Republic of
Azerbaijan Heydar Aliyev, dated January 6, 1994, the Supreme Attestation Commission was
removed from the State Committee for Science and Technology of the Republic of
Azerbaijan and subordinated to the President of the Republic of Azerbaijan and renamed as
the “Supreme Attestation Commission under the President of the Republic of Azerbaijan”.
The Higher Attestation Commission under the President of the Republic of Azerbaijan
(hereinafter, the “Commission”) shall certify highly qualified scientific and scientific -
pedagogical personnel in the Republic, award them the degrees of Doctor of Philosophy and
Doctor of Sciences, and the titles of associate professor and professor in all fields of science,
technology, education, culture and art. It is a state body that provides offical recognition of
the documents of scientific degrees and scientific titles awarded in foreign countries, and
accreditation of scientific institutions (organizations), state policy and regulation in this field,
and participates in the formation of scientific potential. The Commission consists of the
Presidium and the Board. The Presidium is composed of full and corresponding members of
ANAS, doctors of sciences, as well as leading specialists in the fields of science, technology,
education and culture. The Board is composed of the Chairman, Deputy Chairmen, Chief
Scientific Secretary, and the heads of the relevant structural units. The Chairman of the
Commission chairs both the Presidium and the Board of the Commission. Approved by
Decree No. 857 of the President of the Republic of Azerbaijan, dated February 21, 2003, the
“Regulations on the Supreme Attestation Commission under the President of the Republic
of Azerbaijan”, Regulation No. 769 of June 5, 2008 , Amendments and Additions, were
promulgated by Decrees No. 381 of February 15, 2011, No. 1544 of July 21, 2017, and No.
727 of June 11, 2019. In order to ensure the implementation of Decree No. 728 of the
President of the Republic of Azerbaijan dated June 11, 2019 , "On approval of the"
Regulations on the procedure for awarding scientific degrees "and" Regulations on the

===== PDF_PAGE 56 =====
2022 K-Innovation Partnership Program with Azerbaijan
44
procedure for awarding scientific titles " Decisions No. 434, 435 and 436 of November 4,
“Forms of Diplomas for Doctor of Philosophy and Doctor of Sciences”, “Form of Certificates
for Associate Professor and Professor” and “Nostrification of documents on s cientific
degrees and scientific titles issued in foreign countries (determination of equivalence) and
the form of certificates of recognition on the basis of re -attestation”. The Commission
makes decisions independently based on the principles of legality, objectivity, transparency
and compliance with professional and ethical norms.9

④ Intellectual Property Agency
The operation and roles and responsibiities of the Intellectual Property Agency of the
Republic of Azerbaijan are prescribed by Decree No. 222 of the President of the Republic of
Azerbaijan dated July 30, 2018. The Intellectual Property Agency of the Republic of
Azerbaijan was established in accordance with Part 2 of Order No. 5 of the President of the
Republic of Azerbaijan dated April 20, 2018, "On measures to improve management in the
field of consumer market control, standardization, metrology and the protection of
intellectual property rights". the related rights, including rights to data sets and integrated
circuit topologies, rights to inventions, utility models, industrial designs, trademarks and
geographical indications, rights to samples of Azerbaijani folklore (traditional cultural
samples), traditional knowledge and other intangible cultural heritage assets (hereinafter,
“intellectual property rights”), constitute a public legal entity that provides for development,
unified regulation and control in this area and coordinates its activities.10

the Special Design Bureau for Radiophysical Equipment in Ordubad, Nakhchivan
Autonomous Republic. On September 29, 1981, the Space Research Scientific-Production
Union (SPU) under the National Academy of Sciences was established on the basis of the

⑥ State Agency for Public Service and Social Innovations under the President of the

The State Agency for Public Service and Social Innovations under the President of the
Republic of Azerbaijan was established by Decree No. 685 of the President of the Republic
of Azerbaijan dated 13 July 2012.12 The State Agency is the central body of executive power
that, according to the direction of its activities, conducts unified management of the “ASAN”
service centers, coordinates the functions of the employees of the state agencies to act at
those centers, has control of and conducts assessments, manages the integration of the
databases of state agencies, accelerate s the organization of electronic services and
improves the management system in this area. The main tasks of the State Agency are as
follows:
• Unified management of centers and coordination of the activities of employees of
state entities who work at these centers.
• Control of implementation of services provided by the centers based on the
principles of efficiency, transparency, kindness and civility , responsibility and
comfort.

⑦ Small and Medium Business (SMB) Development Agency of the Republic of
Azerbaijan
The Small and Medium Business (SMB) Development Agency of the Republic of Azerbaijan
was established in accordance with the Decree of the President of the Republic of Azerbaijan
dated December 28, 2017. The Agency Charter and structure were endorsed by the Decree
of the President of Azerbaijan dated June 26, 2018.
As a legal entity under the Ministry of Economy, the Agency is authorized to support the
development of small and medium businesses (SMBs) in Azerbaijan by providing them with
a number of services and by ensuring that the services rendered by government entities to
SMBs are properly coordinated and regulated. The Agency leadership comprises seven
members of the Supervision Council, which in turn is composed of the deputy ministers of
Finance, Labor and Social Protection of the Population, and Agriculture, representatives of
the State Tax Service under the Ministry of Economy of the Republic of Azerbaijan, and the
Deputy Chairman of the State Agency for Citizens’ Services and Social Innovation under the
President of the Republic of Azerbaijan and the President of the National Confederation of
Entrepreneurs (Employers) Organizations of Azerbaijan. The Council is chaired by the
Minister of Economy of the Republic of Azerbaijan.13

⑧ Azerbaijan Space Agency (Azercosmos) public legal entity
Established on May 3, 2010 under the full ownership of the government of the Republic of
Azerbaijan, “Azercosmos” is the only satellite operator in the Caucasus. Having launched
Azerspace-1, the first -ever satellite of Azerbaijan, on February 8 , 2013, the company
provides highly reliable, satellite -based communication services to enterprise s and

13 https://smb.gov.az/en/nav/about-us, 07.02.2022

===== PDF_PAGE 60 =====
2022 K-Innovation Partnership Program with Azerbaijan
48
governmental customers for DTH, voice, video, data and mobility applications.
With the advanced design and flexible switching capabilities of the Azerspace -1
telecommunication satellite, millions of viewers are able to gain access to information and
communications services worldwide. Azerspace-1 plays a vital role as a gateway in joint
space that connects more than fifty countries across Central Asia, Europe, Africa and the
Middle East.
In December 2014, Azercosmos took over the rights to operate and commercialize Azersky,
a high-resolution (1.5m imagery products) optical Earth observation satellite, and entered
the commercial business of Earth observation services, including geo-information services.
With its high ly skilled personnel and advanced technical capacity, “Azercosmos” has
succeeded in becoming one of the driving forces of the ICT sector in the country and the
region in the short period since its inception. Its resources have allowed the company to
become well-equipped and hence handle emerging technologies, as well as creating added
value for the general public, that is beyond the reach of the terrestrial networks in benefiting
from the satellite-based services.
The company’s s trategic development plans include enhancing the coverage area and
spectrum of its services. To this end, on September 25, 2018, Azercosmos launched its
second telecommunications satellite, Azerspace-2, in a geostationary orbit of 45° East
longitude.
“Azercosmos” holds an ISO 9001:2008 certificate issued by Bureau Veritas in January 2013.
Azercosmos has focused its Quality and Information Security Policy in order to maintain the
quality of its services and increase information security. This document may be submitted
for acquaintance at the request of a customer or other interested parties.14 The Research
and Development Center at Azercosmos has been established for the purpose of conducting
scientific and experimental research in the field of aerospace, as well as to support the
development and effective exploitation of the space industry in Azerbaijan. The Research
and Development Center is working on the development of software to efficiently operate

The Agency’s remit is to oversee the arrangement and regulation of activities in the field of

also involved in the implementation of state policy and is included in the structure of the

I-Corps is a globally -known US approach to commercializ ing research outputs.
Unfortunately, there are no similar educational programs in Azerbaijan at present .
Instructors and mentors work with start-ups under an ad-hoc based approach. Fortunately,
“Azerbaijan 2026: Social and Economic Development Strategy of the Republic of Azerbaijan
2022-2026” covers certain areas of research commercialization which resemble particular
aspects of NSF I-Corps (though not necessarily similar programs). For example, the third
column of the Strategy aims to support the commercialization of scientific research and R&D
projects and increase the related expenditure to 0.1 percent of GDP, and to increase the
added-value of high- and medium technology industries by up to 15% in terms of aggregate
national added-value by 2026. Moreover, the Strategy will form the “Science-Industry
Cluster 4.0” and increase the participation by 15 percent per year .

Industrial parks
Balakhani Industrial Park 25
“The Complex Plan of Measures for Improvement of the Ecological Situation in the Republic
of Azerbaijan, 2006-2010” was approved by the President of the Republic of Azerbaijan on
September 28, 2006 with the purpose of systematically resolving the existing ecological
problems in the Absheron region. This complex plan of measures has great importance for
enhancing the natural environment around Baku and the Absheron peninsula.
The Complex Plan reflects all major activities aimed at improving the current environmental
situation. As such, it covers all measures and other issues related to improvement of the
ecological situation in Baku Bay, the Bibi-Heybat area, the surrounding area of the Heydar
Aliyev International Airport, the lakes of the Absheron peninsula, oil-contaminated lands,
groundwater-flooded areas and other lands polluted with industrial wastes. The resolution
of issues related to the collection, transportation and placement of solid household waste
in Absheron are among the principal tasks approved in the Complex Plan of Measures.
According to the Decree of President of the Republic of Azerbaijan on “Improvement of the
Management of Solid Household Waste in Baku City” dated August 6, 2008, the collection
and transportation of all kinds of solid household wastes, regardless of their origins, remains
under the competences of Baku City’s executive power. According to the Decree, the newly
established Tamiz Shahar JSC shall “carry out the works related to improvement of the city’s
ecological situation through the placement and disposal o f solid household wastes in
accordance with modern standards”.
The Tamiz Shahar JSC was entered in the state registration on March 12, 2009, whereupon
it commenced its activities. As a state enterprise, it has built its administrative activity
independently, while the supervision of its management and overall activities is carried out

was transferred under the management of Azerbaijan Investment Holding and the following

Tamiz Shahar JSC was entrusted with the management, placement and disposal of
household wastes in accordance with modern standards and in an organized manner, as
well as the task of improving the environmental situation of the city and the development
of this field based on the principles of the market economy.

Sumgait Technologies Park (STP27)
• STP is one of the largest industrial production centers in the region in terms of
surface area.
• Twelve factories constitute an unparalleled complex of specialized production bases,
each one unique in terms of product assortment.
• More than 30 production sites are based within the 250 hectares of the enterprise.
• STP is resident member of the Sumgait Chemical Industrial Park (SCIP).
• A 50-hectare indoor production site is based within the 250 hectares of the
enterprise.
• Twelve plants with more than 30 production sites employ up to 2,000 workers.
• The enterprise stocks more than 40 products not manufactured in the country.
• STP products are well known in Russia, Georgia, Kazakhstan, Kyrgyzstan, and
Turkmenistan, as well as Turkey, for their high quality, and are sold under the brand
“Made in Azerbaijan”.

economic development directions and national priorities, it is essential to create a
modern and effective production, trade and service infrastructure , attract
investments in innovative technologies, realize the region’s industrial potential, and
develop the necessary incentives and entrepreneurship. Furthermore, favourable
conditions must be created to improve the overall environment.

for socio -economic development, future concentration of cargo flows , and the

and innovative industries, as well as enterprises for the production of construction materials
in the Jabrayil region, will be useful in the restoration of other liberated regions.

 Business Incubators and Accelerators
Generally, business incubator and accelerator centers are the kind of venture support
organizations whose main objective is to nurture new businesses and start-ups for further
commercialization in local and international markets. The centers provide all tangible (space,
shared equipment, administrative services) and intangible (knowledge, networking,
investment opportunities) business support services for young entrepreneurs. The Centers
could be funded by the government or the private sector, or they could fund themselves by
charging rental to incubated start-ups and young entrepreneurs.
Techno park of Baku Engineering University 34
The techno park has been operated as part of the Baku Engineering University (BMU) since
2016. So far, about fifty start-ups have moved in here and received developmental support,
and there are ten start-ups in the Incubation Centre.
Purpose of establishment and working principle of the Incubation Center:
The start-up ecosystem in the BMU, including the BMU Techno park; was designed and
established to encourage and support the development of business ideas of students and
teachers, as well as other stakeholders. Although the Incubation Center operates primarily
on the principles of the University (including the BMU Techno park), it is also open to
approaches that meet the needs and requirements of the market and users, as well as
stakeholders.
1) Types of support for accepted start-ups:
Mentoring, networks, seed investment and investors, office conditions (devices, internet,
furniture, etc.), laboratories, libraries, seminars and meeting rooms, access to foreign
markets, training and academic support services, as well as the social opportunities
provided by BMU use, etc.
2) Selection criteria, selection process and sectors:
Innovation and practical significance of the project, the existence and determination of the

34 https://beu.edu.az/az/page/texnopark-30, 09.02.2022.

===== PDF_PAGE 73 =====
Chapter 3. Current Status of the Technology Commercialization System and Programs in Azerbaijan
61
team, the potential value-added potential of the claim, market access potential, etc.
3) Involvement of start-ups in the Incubation Center is carried out in two directions:
• New ideas are proposed for the development of start-up competition winners.
• Start-ups are accepted on the basis of direct applications.

ADA Innovation Center 35
The ADA Innovation Center (ADA Innovation Center) was officially launched as a type of
educational institution on April 15, 2017, with the aim of contributing to the development
of Start-ups and Small Business in Azerbaijan. The center will first provide services to ADA
students and then to other students. Currently, there is an office area where five teams can
work. Since 2017, the ADA Ideas Competition has been held each year and prizes are
awarded for 1st, 2nd and 3rd places. Teams for the Innovation Center are also selected
through the Ideas Competition.
1) Purpose of establishment and working principle of the Incubation Center:
The Incubation Center’s remit is to strengthen the practical potential and activities of
students, create opportunities for the application of knowledge, prepare students for the
real business and industrial sector, and help them to build their education and relationships
for the purpose of starting a business. During the academic year, training programs are held
to develop students’ business, IT and other practical skills ; the Idea s competition is
announced and the winners are announced in the 2nd semester of the academic year; and
students are introduced to sponsors and provided with opportunities to share ideas through
the ADA Foundation.
2) Types of support for accepted start-ups:
Winners of the Ideas Competition receive diverse benefits including cash prizes, assistance
with preparing for competitions at home and abroad, opportunities to participate in various
training programs free of charge or at low rates, access to office facilities at the Innovation
Center, direct access to key members of the Start-up and Entrepreneurship ecosystem at

35 https://www.ada.edu.az/en, 09.02.2022

===== PDF_PAGE 74 =====
2022 K-Innovation Partnership Program with Azerbaijan
62
home and abroad, networking events/media support, a one-on-one presentation to ADA
University teachers as needed, and recognition of the Center's work as an official internship
to recruit resident teams registered at the Innovation Center, etc.
3) Selection criteria, selection process and sectors:
Selection is generally carried out through the Ideas Competition. However, teams that do
not participate in the competition but show good results at partner centers are accepted.
The criteria are mainly based on Business (Ability to Bring Money) and Innovation, and
include selection from various fields (Sales, Marketing, IT, Networking, Design, etc.) One
Academic Jury and one Industrial Jury are appointed to review the performances of the
teams and determine the winners by secret ballot. There is no private sector option.

INNOLAND36
INNOLAND is an incubation, acceleration and research center that was established to
develop the private sector, promote innovation and expand the start-up movement in
Azerbaijan and beyond its borders. INNOLAND performs its activities under the State Agency
for Services to Citizens and Social Innovations (ASAN Service) under the President of the
republic of Azerbaijan.
“INNOLAND” consists of an incubator, an accelerator, a co-working space and an IT Training
and Education Center. The IT Training and Education Center is a place where young people
acquire programming, IT and coding skills.
The mission and working principle of the Center are as follows:
• Foster a spirit of entrepreneurial innovation and promote the start-up ecosystem.
• Create an environment that inspires people to think differently.
• Establish a role model and empower entrepreneurs with the tools and community
needed to grow and master their innovative businesses.

INNOLAND, an Incubation and Acceleration Center, is designed to allow anyone who has a

36 http://innoland.az/en/home/activity

===== PDF_PAGE 75 =====
Chapter 3. Current Status of the Technology Commercialization System and Programs in Azerbaijan
63
good idea to realize their idea, as well as to develop the private sector, encourage
innovation and expand the start-up movement. The types of support available for start-ups
are as follows:
• Incubation stage : Residents receive the international experience of our mentors
from the early stages of their formation and development.
• Acceleration stage: Residents undergo an intensive acceleration program designed
to grow and develop start-ups in Azerbaijan and help them to enter international
markets.
• Co-working stage : Tangible and intangible services are provided to physical or
virtual residents. (The co-working environment is available 24/7 under the Virtual
Residency program.)
• Education stage: Residents receive training sessions and programs on IT skills, such
as coding, programming, and information technologies.

The partners of INNOLAND Center are active members of the innovation ecosystem of
Azerbaijan, namely, the Next Step Innovation Center, SUP VC, Tech Academy, Start-up
Azerbaijan, Start-up Estonia, Youth Inc. Entrepreneurship Program, etc. All the mentioned
partners provide intensive contributions at different stages of the entire innovation process
of nurturing start-ups and young entrepreneurs.
In 2021, at the initiative of the Innovations Center of the ASAN Service and AREA (Azerbaijan
Robotics Engineering Academy), the INNOLAND AREA center was introduced to the
innovation ecosystem of Azerbaijan with the aim of supporting the formation of an
ecosystem that will ensure the development of start-ups in the regions and of strengthening
the favourable conditions in order to increase regional potential.
The key activity consists in organizing training sessions in the fields of engineering and
robotics for children and young people aged 4-19 living in the regions, and in developing
their competences in the fields of entrepreneurship, business, innovation, start-up, project
management, etc.

2. Intellectual Property System

 Basic Legislation
There is no specific legislation on technology transfer and commercialization in Azerbaijan;
however, the actual legal basis on IP37 consists of the following laws of the Republic of
Azerbaijan:
① Law on Patents, Law on Trademarks and Geographical Indications;
② Law on the Legal Protection of Topographies of Integrated Circuits;
③ Law on Copyrights and Other Related Rights; and
④ Law on the Legal Protection of Data Compilations.

Although the activities of different organizations (ANAS, Intellectual Property Agency,
Ministry of Digital Development and Communication, Ministry of Economy, etc.) are
governed by different legislative acts, there are no specific provisions on technology transfer
and commercialization – a lacuna which creates certain difficulties in the field of technology
transfer and commercialization.
The following legislative acts can be regarded as legislative acts on the partial
commercialization of technologies:
① Decree of the President of the Republic of Azerbaijan on ensuring coordination in
the field of innovative development in the Republic of Azerbaijan;
② Law of the Republic of Azerbaijan on science;
③ Rules for maintaining the registration of residents of technology business
incubators, as well as th e register of residents of technology parks and residents
of technology business incubators ;
④ Form for registration certificates of technology park s and technology business
incubators;

37 https://www.ip-coster.com/IPGuides/c/intellectual-property-azerbaijan

===== PDF_PAGE 81 =====
Chapter 3. Current Status of the Technology Commercialization System and Programs in Azerbaijan
69
⑤ Resolution of the Cabinet of Ministers of the Republic of Azerbaijan on approval
of the “Application form for registration as a resident of a technology park and the
list of attached documents”;
⑥ Resolution of the Cabinet of Ministers of the Republic of Azerbaijan on approval
of the “Criteria for determining start-ups”;
⑦ Azerbaijan 2030: National Priorities for Socio-Economic Development; and
⑧ Decree of the President of the Republic of Azerbaijan on ensuring the activity of
the Innovation Agency (current name: Agency for Innovation and Digital

Articles Contents
Article 1.
General
Provisions
The Innovations Agency (currently, the Agency for Innovation and Digital
Transformation) under the Ministry of Transport, Communications and High
T echnologies of the Republic of Azerbaijan (hereinafter, the Ministry) engages in
innovation activities, provides assistance to local businesses in obtaining modern
technologies and technological solutions and their transfer , and supports
innovative research. It is a public legal entity operating in the field of project
promotion (including start-ups), financing with grants, investment (60% / 40%,
respectively) with authorized capital (including venture financing), innovation
initiatives (hereinafter, the relevant field), and control and regulation.
As the Innovations Agency has been dissolved and merged with the Agency for
Innovation and Digital Transformation, there is no indicative budget for grants or
investments. Such information will soon be available upon approval of its legal
mandate and establishment. So far , the Agency has established a venture capital
fund with the private sector and its indicative budget is about 10 million US
dollars.

 Legislation of Intellectual Property
2.2.1 Current status of intellectual property
The Constitution of the Republic of Azerbaijan enshrines copyrights, the right of invention
and other types of intellectual property rights (Article 30 of the Constitution), and provides
full legal guarantees of creative freedom (Article 51 of the Constitution). At the same time,
serious reforms have been carried out to ensure those rights and, in general, copyrights and
other intellectual property rights have been accepted as a priority of the state. During this
same period, the government has established the Copyright Agency, whose legal status is
equal to that of a central executive body, and passed the following five laws providing legal
regulation in the field of copyrights, related rights and other similar intellectual property
rights (as well as taking important steps to ensure collective management of the property
rights of authors):
• Law on Copyrights and Other Related Rights;
• Law on the Legal Protection of Integrated Circuit Topologies;
• Law on the Legal Protection of Data Sets";
• Law on the Legal Protection of Samples of Azerbaijani Folklore;
• Law on the Protection of Intellectual Property Rights and the Fight against Piracy .
• Law on Copyrights and Other Related Rights;
• Law on the Legal Protection of Integrated Circuit Topologies;
• Law on the Legal Protection of Data Sets";
• Law on the Legal Protection of Samples of Azerbaijani Folklore;
• Law on the protection of intellectual property rights and fight against piracy
adopted, important steps have been taken to ensure the collective management of
property rights of authors.

Azerbaijan, which has successfully entered the international intellectual property system, is
now also a party to the Berne Convention for the Protection of Literary and Artistic Works
under the auspices of the World Intellectual Property Organization (WIPO), as well as to

===== PDF_PAGE 83 =====
Chapter 3. Current Status of the Technology Commercialization System and Programs in Azerbaijan
71
Conventions on the Protection of the Rights of Performers, Phonogram Producers, and
Broadcasting Organizations, the WIPO Internet Contracts on Copyright and the Records of
Performances and Phonograms. It has also acceded to the UNESCO "World Authorship
Agreement" and the "Convention for the Safeguarding of the Intangible Cultural Heritage".
At the same time, serious steps have been taken to form a digital copyright system within a
“knowledge-based economy” that meets the ultra-modern digital challenges facing the
country. The structure of the system's technical support has been changed, especially in the
information society, i.e. the "knowledge-based creative economy", while the situation
within the digital copyright space has been analysed and studied, and a digital rights
management system and e -services have all been established. Initially adopted by the
Cabinet of Ministers of the Republic of Azerbaijan in December 2011 as a part of the
formation of a more comprehensive copyrights system, the Center for Ensuring Intellectual
Property Rights was established under the auspices of the Copyright Agency, and began to
operate within the WIPO Academy.
The results of the successful economic reforms of our country have created the need to
calculate the share of industry (creative industry) in terms of gross domestic product (GDP)
based on copyright s and other related rights. Calculated using a special methodology
developed by the Agency and based on international experience, this share rose from 2.1%
in 2003 to 5% at the beginning of 2015, and is now at the level of developed countries. It
should be noted that Azerbaijan is one of the first countries in the CIS to adopt the voluntary
official registration of creative works. In order to automate the registration of works, the
Agency has built an electronic database "Registration" service, as well as a digital online
registration system and a digital library of Azerbaijani folklore.
As of the beginning of 2016, some 10,350 works were registered with the Agency, and
information about those works and their authors (right holders) was loaded into the
“Registration” electronic database. The creation of a national information system in the field
of copyrights, ensuring its international exchange, registration of works and objects of
related rights, data sets protected by the right of special protection, integrated circuit
topologies and electronic database of right holders application and regular updates are

===== PDF_PAGE 84 =====
2022 K-Innovation Partnership Program with Azerbaijan
72
provided. In order to ensure the implementation of the Decree of the President of the
Republic of Azerbaijan "On measures for organization of the e-services of government
agencies", dated May 23, 2011, a special section named "Electronic Services" was created
on the Agency’s website, on which samples of application forms were posted, and
administrative regulations for the provision of services were prepared and approved. Works
are registered online via electronic services.

“Intellectual property right” refers to the exclusive right of a citizen or a legal entity to the
results of its intellectual creativity, or equivalent products, types of work and services (firm
name, trademark, service mark, etc.) in the cases and in the manner prescribed by law.
“Intellectual property rights” refers to the rights to copyrighted works, performances,
phonograms, broadcasts, integrated circuit topologies, data sets, folklore (traditional
cultural samples), inventions, utility models, industrial designs, trademarks, and
geographical indications.
The Law of the Republic of Azerbaijan on the Enforcement of Intellectual Property Rights
and Combating Piracy regulates the relations related to guaranteeing the protection of
intellectual property rights, the protection of the interests of holders of intellectual property
rights, and the prevention of all forms of illegal production and the imposition of sanctions
and penalties in this field, including the copying of intellectual properties .

 Legislation of Industrial Property Rights
THE LAW OF THE REPUBLIC OF AZERBAIJAN ON PATENTS regulates property relations and
personal non-property relations arising in connection with the creation, legal protection and
use of inventions, utility models and industrial designs in the territory of the Republic of
Azerbaijan. The main provisions of the law are as follows:
Article 13. Employer and author
① The owner of the enterprise or the head of the enterprise, his authorized body, as
well as engaged in entrepreneurial activity not prohibited by the Law of the
Azerbaijan Republic “On Entrepreneurial Activity”, regardless of the organizational

===== PDF_PAGE 87 =====
Chapter 3. Current Status of the Technology Commercialization System and Programs in Azerbaijan
75
legal form in which the author's employee (hereinafter - the employee) works;
Individuals who are considered employers. [32]
② The right to obtain a patent for an invention, utility model or industrial design
created by an employee in connection with the performance of official duties or a
written assignment (production, research, artistic -graphic and other type) given
by the employer, the contract between the employer and the employee if not, it
belongs to the employer. The relationship between the employer and the
employee regarding the newly created invention, utility model and industrial
design shall be regulated according to the terms of the contract.
③ An employee(s) who has created an invention, utility model or industrial design in
accordance with the foregoing paragraph 2 of this Article shall submit a written
notice to the employer within 1 month of its creation.
④ In the event that an employer refuses to file a claim for an invention, utility model
or industrial design, or does not notify the employee concerned of his or her
intention to exercise this right within 3 months from the date of receipt of the
notification, then this right shall pass to the employee. Prior to filing the claim, the
employer and the employee shall not disclose the nature of the invention, utility
model or industrial design.
⑤ In accordance with the foregoing paragraph 4 of this article, within 6 months from
the date of receipt of the patent, the employer shall have the pre-emptive right to
use the patent under the license agreement.
⑥ In all other cases, except for th ose cases provided in paragraph 2 of this Article,
the right to obtain a patent for an invention, utility model or industrial design
created by an employee shall belong to the employee.
⑦ The employer shall pay the employee the appropriate fee in accordance with the
terms of the contract concluded between them for the creation of an invention,
utility model or industrial design. In the event that the employer assigns the right
to use such invention, utility model or industrial design to another person, the
employee shall have the right to demand an increase in the fee if the income
received by the employer does not correspond to the amount of remuneration

===== PDF_PAGE 88 =====
2022 K-Innovation Partnership Program with Azerbaijan
76
paid to the employee, or if the employer continues to participate in the use of the
facility. An employee has the right to receive an additional payment if the use of
an invention, utility model or industrial design yields more economic benefits than
provided for in the contract concluded between the employer and the employee.
If the employer decides to waive the rights to a patent for an invention, utility
model or industrial design, the employer shall offer the employee in advance a
contract for the transfer of rights to the invention, utility model and industrial
design.
⑧ When the relevant executive authority is a customer of special research or design
work related to the national security of the state, the relationships pertaining to
inventions, utility models or industrial designs created by the executor in
connection with the performance of these works shall be regulated by the Law on
State Secrets of the Azerbaijan Republic.[33]
⑨ In the event of a d ispute between an employer and an employee concerning an
invention, utility model or industrial design, it shall be settled administratively and
in court. [34]
