In the fall of 2021, rather than funding two separate types of awards, Nodes and Sites, to
support the National Innovation Network, NSF started to fund Hubs. This new, more
integrated award (NSF Solicitation 20 -529) incorporates the lessons learned from the
program’s initial success and represents an operational change to the way institutions will
receive funding from the NSF. The new operational model provides increased and level
(rather than previously declining) funding, and is more scalable, to support the expansion of
I-Corps.
[Figure 4-32] New I-Corps Hubs of the NSF

Source: NSF (2021)

===== PDF_PAGE 219 =====
Chapter 4. Policy Recommendations
207
The I-Corps Hubs will serve national needs in the following five areas of responsibility:
① Create and implement tools, resources and training activities that enhance the
nation's innovation capacity.
② Identify, develop, and support promising research that can generate economic
value.
③ Gather, analyse, evaluate, and utilize data and insights resulting from the
experiences of those participating in local, regional, and national I -Corps
programs.
④ Provide opportunities to diverse communities of innovators.
⑤ Share and leverage effective innovation practices on a national scale to improve
the quality of life across the nation.
The NSF plans to issue a new I -Corps Hubs solicitation in the fall of 2021 to continue to
expand opportunities for entrepreneurship education and technology translation
throughout the United States. The five I-Corps Hubs announced today, and the participating
institutions, are listed below.

The I-Corps Hubs build on the lessons learned during the first decade of the I-Corps program.
Specifically, in the program's initial phase, I -Corps Nodes, consortia of universities
responsible for delivering a standardized curriculum, and I -Corps Sites, universities
providing internal institutional support to scientists and engineers, were funded separately.
Under the new approach, the I -Corps Hubs offer a more integrated approach that
incorporates the lessons learned from the program's initial success. The new operational
model provides increased funding and enables the scaling of I-Corps across the entire

===== PDF_PAGE 222 =====
2022 K-Innovation Partnership Program with Azerbaijan
210
country. The NSF-supported Sites and Nodes will continue to participate in the National
Innovation Network while the NSF transitions to the Hubs.
In Korea, the I-Corps program was started by the Ministry of Science and ICT (now renamed
as the Min istry of Science and Technology ). The Ministry of Science and Technology
introduced I-Corps, a representative education program for science and technology start-
ups, following negotiations with the US NSF in 2014.
Starting with the two base universities of KAIST and POSTECH in 2015, the program was
expanded to four base universities with the addition of Korea University and Sungkyunkwan
University in 2018, while GIST, UNIST, and Ewha Womans University (a base university
specializing in advanced education courses for women) were added in 2021.
The 7 I-Corps Nodes in Korea
• Northern metropolitan and Gangwon Node: Korea University
• Southern metropolitan Node: Seongkyun-kwan University
• Chungcheong Node: KAIST
• Honam Node: GIST
• Daegu and Gyeongsangbuk-do Node: POSTECH
• Gyeongnam Node: UNIST
• Women-specific Node: Ewha Womans University

Chapter 4. Policy Recommendations

[Figure 4-33] Korean I-Corps - Regional Node Universities

Source: NRF (2022)

After becoming operational, the I-Corps program was operated under the current seven
Node universities, training a total of 148 universities and 11 government research institutes’
start-up preliminary teams (including duplicates), and ultimately establishing itself as a
representative science and technology start-up education program of Korean universities.
[Figure 4-34] Lab to Start-up Boot Camp

Source: Korea Entrepreneurship Foundation, 2021.

===== PDF_PAGE 224 =====
2022 K-Innovation Partnership Program with Azerbaijan
212
In Korea, a total of 435 teams have completed the course since 2015, the year in which the
Korean-style I-Corps project started, including 125 teams selected in 2021, and an additional
137 teams have been selected for the first time in 2022. Regarding the start-up rate, 186
(42.7%) of the 435 teams that completed the training have established start-up companies,
which is close to the 50% rate of the NSF I-Corps in the United States.
Of those start-up companies, 122 start-up companies, about 65.5%, have survived, showing
excellent performances of about 70% for the 3-year survival rate and 44.5% for the 5-year
survival rate. This can be interpreted as a better performance than the 5-year survival rate
of Korean start-ups, which is 31.2% (Statistics Office, 2020).
In terms of job creation, analysis has shown that 1,003 new jobs were created, with an
average of 5.4 people being hired by each start-up company. In the case of instructors
(Korea I -Corps Instructors), from 2017 to 2022, about eight I-Corps Instructors were
nurtured every year under an agreement with George Washington University in the United
States, and, as of 2022, a total of about forty-six I-Corps Instructors have the ability to lead
a Korean technology start-up team. The figures shown in the table below are based on the
findings of a report on a comparative study of the performance of the K orea I -Corps
Program with that of the US I-Corps program from 2017 to 2020 (Shim et al., 2022).

3.1.6 Structure of the Korea I-Corps program for R&D Start-ups
The government promotion department of the I-Corps project in Korea is the Ministry of
Science, Technology and Information and Communication. Regarding the operation of
domestic education programs, the Korea Youth Entrepreneurship Foundation (KoEF) is in
charge of domestic education, and in the case of overseas education, KIC D.C operates
overseas education courses in connection with George Washing ton University and UC
Berkeley University in the United States.

3.1.7 I-Corps program localization for the Korea region
The seven-week U.S. NSF I -Corps training program is largely composed of basic theory
education, discovery of customers, inferring implications based on the results of customer
discovery (lessons learned), and coaching with a dedicated team mentor (office hours).
Upon completion of the 7-week course, the program is promoted in such a way that
excellent teams are selected through an IR pitching-type start-up competition.
The Korean I-Corps education program mainly consists of a boot camp, a pre-session, a basic
course, and a practical course conducted over a period of 2 months.
In the case of laboratory start-up programs in Korea, the I-Corps trademark cannot be used
officially. This is because the original program, named I-Corps, is an educational program
created by the taxpayers of the United States. Therefore, in Korea, the official name is a
“public technology-based market-linked start-up search support project”, and in the case of
the US I -Corps program, the name “Tech Frontier” is officially used. However, in this
research report, the Korean program also uses the name I -Corps informally to help the
reader to understand the start-up program.

Chapter 4. Policy Recommendations

Furthermore, non -face-to-face education methods were introduced in addition to the
existing face -to-face education al methods during the COVID -19 pandemic in 2020.
improved through interviews.
The activity of the Korean-style I-Corps education program can be defined as customer
discovery in the same way as in the United States. Customer discovery is the process of
finding out whether a business hypothesis established by a technology-based start-up team
is meaningful to the target customer segment in the real-world market through in-depth
interviews with real people.
With the Korean I-Corps program, as in the US, 100 customer discovery interviews are held,
i.e. 15 for the Basic Course, an additional 20 between the Basic Course and the Practical
Course, and 65 for the Practical Course. These 100 interviews are a process of finding out
whether the R&D and laboratory technology possessed by the technology start-up team is
an urgent and important issue for segmented customers, and is usually referred to as
“Product Market Fit” (PMF). In addition, approaches to solving the problems of potential
target customers, with the focus on customers, are taught at the same time. This is a process
of exploring the application fields of a laboratory technology as a solution to the problems
of potential customers, and is commonly called “Problem Solution Fit” (PSF)
[Figure 4-37] Korea I-Corps customer discovery, KoEF (2021)

Source: Korea Entrepreneurship Foundation (2021)

===== PDF_PAGE 232 =====
2022 K-Innovation Partnership Program with Azerbaijan
220
The main content is the current Korean content, which includes detailed laboratory start-
up education in the educational process, whereby students who have majored in
engineering are first familiarized with the I -Corps model of hypothesis testing and the
market exploration in-depth interview, etc., and then learn about the market validation
process. It is necessary to operate through the flip -learning method as an after -sales
education, which is a stage that can be accepted through the verification of business
hypotheses, and some curriculum systems have been developed in a way that combines the
advantages of online and offline methods, which were promoted in the past through policy
research related to the end of 2021. Since 2022, it has been operated in five stages as follows:

 “Train the Trainer” start-up program for Azerbaijan
3.2.1 Train the Trainer(TTT) program
In the case of Korea, in order to internalize the Korean-style I-Corps program launched in
2015, Korean start-up experts have been dispatched to George Washington University, a
representative NSF I-Corps Node University in the United States, for education since 2017.
Instructors conduct interviews in Korean and English after reviewing documents in advance,
but applicants must have at least three years of educational experience related to start-up
and professional activities, such as consulting and mentoring in the field of start-ups. The
evaluation committee is composed of experts in the field of public technology
commercialization, and the interview evaluation is conducted based on the applicant's
understanding of I -Corps. Through the Korean I -Corps program, approximately 6 -8
instructors with outstanding educational capabilities have been selected each year since
2017, and a total of 46 will be produced by 2022. The selected Korean I-Corps instructors
participate in the Pre-session and Basic Course in Korea immediately after being selected,
thereby deepening their understanding of the basic I-Corps curriculum program and their
basic experience in guiding a start-up team. Upon completing the education course in Korea,
the participants attend the Instructors Course at George Washington University (US), in
which the NSF I -Corps instructors participate, for about three weeks. At this time, the
instructors on the U.S. curriculum conduct customer discovery, which the actual start-up
team experiences, in two -cohort teams of four people. The main curriculum can be
summarized as follows:

===== PDF_PAGE 234 =====
2022 K-Innovation Partnership Program with Azerbaijan
222
[Figure 4-38] TTT program, George Washington University, KIC D.C (2019)

Source: KIC D.C. (2019)

The core of the training process is customer discovery of the Lean Start-up method, which
repeatedly tests business hypotheses in the market. In addition, various online-based tools
are used in the curriculum, of which the most characteristic element consists in using a
Cloud-based Web Solution called LaunchPad ( https://www.glidr.io/launchpad#
accelerators). Through this, feedback on the customer discovery results and points to be
noted during the training process can be shared in real time during the training course run
by the US instructor. In particular, LaunchPad provides video-recorded lectures (mostly on
Lean Start-up methodologies, customer discovery, interview tips) by Professor Steve Blank
of Stanford University, who designed the NSF I-Corps program. The lectures are linked to
each Business Model 9 Block, and feedback on the results of customer discovery is provided
simultaneously by multiple mentors. In addition, all the participants can communicate with
each other via LaunchPad’s collaborative features, which allow them to receive and/or send
a messages.

3.2.2 “Train the Trainer” (TTT) program syllabus for Korean Instructors
GW (George Washington University) Mentor Networking Event: A session based around
personal exchanges and networking between Korean instructors, American instructors, and
mentors.

Faculty Development Day 1.: Training on the basic mind-set, lean start-up methodology, and
Business Model Canvas (BMC) required of instructors.

===== PDF_PAGE 236 =====
2022 K-Innovation Partnership Program with Azerbaijan
224

Class 1, Course Opening Workshop: The key concepts of the BMC 9 blocks are taught in
detail in the form of a lecture, and learners are encouraged to participate by taking part in
quizzes from time to time.

===== PDF_PAGE 237 =====
Chapter 4. Policy Recommendations
225
Class 2, Team Presentation, Workshop, Assignment: A team called Cohort is formed to give
a team presentation on business items that will be used for customer discovery in advance
for each team. In addition to the BMC 9 Blocks, through the topic of advanced topics,
training on issues that the start -up team should consider in order to verify business
hypotheses other than BMC is conducted in the form of a workshop. Each team is given a
task to complete, and approximately ten customer discovery interviews are conducted
during a given period (the tasks are usually presented at intervals of 3 days to 1 week).

Customer discovery interview: Customer discovery is not about selling the skills the Cohort
team possesses; rather, it is about designing an interview that can help people to identify
customer pain or customer gain points in their minds.

===== PDF_PAGE 238 =====
2022 K-Innovation Partnership Program with Azerbaijan
226

Office hours: After customer discovery, teams receive coaching through Mentor and online
meeting tools such as Zoom or WebEx. Helping to become customer discovery thereof.

Class 3, Team Presentation, What’s Next: The above training courses are repeated each
week. When participants move on to the second half, they proceed with the process of
pushing to complete the Product Market Fit or Problem Solution Fit of their assignment.

Final presentation and completion ceremony.

format, but the following textbooks are recommended as reference materials for TTT
trainees.

 Implications and Policy Suggestions
3.3.1 Suggestion for enhancing Azerbaijan’s start-up eco-systems
As was the case with Korea's industrialization growth process, to create an innovative
ecosystem in Azerbaijan, it will be necessary to strengthen its IP R&D capabilities in order to
develop public technologies that fit the strategic direction of the country. In addition, after
securing intellectual properties and technologies that are suited to the regional and
industrial characteristics of Azerbaijan, the implementation of I-Corps-type technology
start-up education to build an innovative ecosystem comprising start-ups will also be helpful
in promoting socio-structural change.

3.3.2 Industry fields for Korean-Azerbaijan start-up cooperation through
ODA projects
Boss (www.boss.az) is the largest local job posting site. According to Vadim, it is not easy for
foreign nationals to start a local business given the poor business environment in Azerbaijan,
including the closed economic structure and the language barrier. However, in view of the
recent development of the tourism industry and the good image of Korea, it is considered
that tourism and trade could be promising fields for start-ups in the short term.
① Tourism: As the number of Korean tourists to the three countries of the Caucasus
increases, the demand for local travel services will increase accordingly.
② Trading industry: Demand for Korean products is steady due to the good level of
brand recognition of Korea, and the import business of Azerbaijani fruits and
vegetables is also promising. However, as the poor business environment delays
the introduction of advanced management know -how and services, certain
common business item s in other countries c ould become new and profitable
business items here. If sufficient market research and preparation is undertaken,
local start-ups could also be promising.
③ Recently opened household goods stores such as Miniso are operating with great
success, and a substitute driver is operating at the suggestion of an international
student who studied in Korea.

===== PDF_PAGE 241 =====
Chapter 4. Policy Recommendations
229
④ Recently, Hezar Dalga TV has begun to air K orean TV dramas during prime time
(9pm), and exports of related Hallyu products are expected to increase.

3.3.3 “Train the trainer ” start-up program for Azerbaijan via the K-
Innovation program
I-Corps is a globally -known US approach to commercializ ing research outputs.
Unfortunately, there are no similar educational programs in Azerbaijan as yet, so instructors
and mentors work with start-ups with an ad-hoc based approach. Fortunately, “Azerbaijan
2026: Social and Economic Development Strategy of the Republic of Azerbaijan in 2022-
2026” covers certain areas of research commercialization which resemble particular aspects
of NSF I-Corps (though they are not necessarily similar programs).
For example, the third pillar of the Strategy aims to support the commercialization of
scientific research and R&D projects and increase such expenditures up to 0.1 percent of
Azerbaijan’s GDP, as well as to increase the added-value of high- and medium technology
industries up to 15% in terms of aggregate national added-value by 2026. Furthermore, the
Strategy will form the Science-Industry 4.0 Cluster and increase the level of participation by
15 percent per year .
Therefore, as in the case of Korea, promoting innovative technology-based start-ups will be
useful in promoting changes in the industrial structure and innovation as a major policy.
In this regard, it is recommended as a good short-term direction to transfer the I-Corps TTT
(Train the Trainer) course and the related experience currently being accumulated in Korea
to train professors at major universities in Azerbaijan and instructors at major start -up
institutions. In this way, it should be possible to nurture instructors who have learned the I-
Corps type of educational methodology in a relatively short period of time. It is expected
that Azerbaijan's own I-Corps program education and operation system will be prepared by
directly operating the training course in the form of co -teaching with a few Korean
instructors for the locally discovered technology start-up team in the initial stages.
In the event that we co-develop Azerbaijan’s own instructors who can teach the I-Corps

===== PDF_PAGE 242 =====
2022 K-Innovation Partnership Program with Azerbaijan
230
methodology, it is expected that it will be possible to operate a program that can
autonomously advance technology start-ups and idea-based start-ups even after the end of
the ODA program.

 Expected Impacts
3.4.1 Impact on building up the R&D based start-up training and eco -
system
The I-Corps project in Korea can be evaluated as having contributed to an important
opportunity to transform the undergraduate -centred idea-based start-up culture into
laboratory start-ups centred on the major hub Node universities in many universities. In
terms of its specific and quantitative performance, Korea's I -Corps program is a
representative technology start-up program. Among the laboratory start-up search teams
that have fostered some 325 teams nationwide, 161 start-ups (49.5%) and 723 new jobs
have been created (4.6 people per company on average); the 3 -year survival rate of
companies started in the first phase is about 70%, the 5-year survival rate is 53.8%, and the
average survival rate as of the current year is 75.8%. Analysed to have created a distinct
innovative performance. As such, the project has been evaluated as having achieved a high
degree of innovative technologies.
This means that the technology efforts of university labs developed with the government's
R&D budget have shifted from the output of patents, theses, and prototypes, which can be
called “research achievements”, to actual laboratory start-ups, which can be defined as
“outcomes”. As such, i t can be considered that a foothold has been established. If this
program is transferred to Azerbaijan, it could present an important opportunity to establish
a technology start-up ecosystem.

===== PDF_PAGE 243 =====
Chapter 4. Policy Recommendations
231
3.4.2 Fostering R&D based start-up instructors and a mentoring pool
As mentioned above, in the case of Korea, instructors trained in teaching and learning
methods through major NSF I-Corps node universities in the United States are intensively
nurtured from the beginning of the project in order to spread the technology start-up
ecosystem through I -Corps. As such, i nstructors who have learned the I -Corps-type
educational methodology can be nurtured in a relatively short period of time. It is expected
that Azerbaijan's own I-Corps program education and operation system will be prepared by
directly operating the training course in the form of co-teaching with Korean instructors for
the locally discovered technology start-up team. In the event that we co -develop
Azerbaijan’s own instructors to teach the I-Corps methodology, it is expected that it will be
possible to operate a program that can autonomously advance technology start-ups and
idea-based start-ups even after the ODA program ends.

 Conclusion
As was the case with Korea's industrialization process, to create an innovative ecosystem in
Azerbaijan, it is necessary to strengthen its IP R&D capabilities in order to develop public
technologies that are suited to the strategic direction of the country. Belongs with the
intellectual properties and technology capabilities suited to the regional and industrial
characteristics of Azerbaijan, I-Corps-type technology start-up education that can build an
innovative ecosystem using start-ups will be helpful in pr omoting social and structural
change. As with the I-Corps project in Korea, it could be evaluated as contributing to an
important opportunity to transform the undergraduate-centred idea-based start-up culture
into laboratory start-ups centred on the major hub Node University in many universities.
This means that the technology work done by university labs with the government's R&D
budget will shift from the output of patents, theses, and prototypes, which can be defined
as “research achievements”, to actual laboratory start-ups, which can be defined as
“outcomes”. In this way it can be considered that a foothold has been established. If this
program is transferred to Azerbaijan, it is believed that it will provide an important
opportunity to establish a technology start-up ecosystem. To achieve this type of goal and

in teaching and learning methods through major NSF I-Corps node universities in the United

Therefore, as in the case of Korea, promoting innovative technology-based start-ups will be

1. Strategic Plan for Improving the Technology
Commercialization System

 Technology commercialization system and its management
A large number of decisions (laws, decrees, orders, acts, etc.) have been adopted with the
aim of establishing the knowledge economy and the innovation ecosystem in the Republic
of Azerbaijan. However, the legislative mechanism and resources for supporting the
organization and development of industrially relevant scientific research, as well as the
related science commercialization processes, are still weak. In this respect, it should be
noted that the ecosystem in Azerbaijan is currently in the initial) stage of formation.
In the Republic of Azerbaijan, there are diverse organizational infrastructures (higher
education institutions, research institutes, technological parks, agro-parks, business and
industrial enterprises, business incubation and acceleration centers, laboratories, etc.) that
directly play an important role in the innovation ecosystem. However, it should be noted
that the commercialization of science and technology transfer mechanisms among the
operational mechanisms of the infrastructural facilities do not fu lly meet current and
prospective expectations. However, the activities carried out for the purpose of developing
intellectual capital are becoming more and more relevant, thus allowing us to make
optimistic predictions about the innovation ecosystem of Aze rbaijan, including the
commercialization of science.
All sides of (participants in the innovation ecosystem in Azerbaijan (scientific-pedagogical,
management, market, educators and learners, etc.) are sympathetic to innovative
Chapter 5. Strategic Plans for
Improving the Technology
Commercialization System and
Developing the HTP Program

===== PDF_PAGE 248 =====
2022 K-Innovation Partnership Program with Azerbaijan
236
approaches. In our opinion, the main problems here are the lack of mass to show stability
and durability, as well as tolerance to the processes of formation of innovations as value.
The occurrence of such situations in the formative stage of the ecosystem can be seen in
the global experience as a whole.
The Republic of Korea provides support (both financial and intellectual) for the formation
and development of an ecosystem to promote technology transfer and the
commercialization of science in Azerbaijan. These forms of support are based on the
advanced experience of Korea, and are implemented after taking into account the needs of
the local market in Azerbaijan. We can note that the support provided through the ODA,
KOICA and other programs can benefit the economy of Azerbaijan, especially the innovation
ecosystem.

 Strengths and Weaknesses of Technology Commercialization
1.2.1 Strengths
• In the last decade, especially in the last few years, the relevance of the issue has
increased in the private sector and at the state level.
• The population is interested in investing in education and professional development.
In particular, families prefer their children to receive a high level of education.
• Adolescents and young people make up about 35% of the total population.
• The new generation shows great interest in smart technologies.
• Even with small achievements in technology transfer and the commercialization of
science, there are opportunities to gain fame.
• The country has currency and resources.
• The population has a high interest in business entrepreneurship.

===== PDF_PAGE 249 =====
Chapter 5. Strategic Plans for Improving the Technology Commercialization
System and Developing the HTP Program
237
1.2.2 Weaknesses
• Technology transfer and commercialization of science are still not priorities in the
education system.
• Free market opportunities are weak.
• Macroeconomic indicators are dominated by a resource-based economy.
• Public condemnation has a negative effect on entrepreneurship.
• The legislation and financial resources supporting technology transfer and science
commercialization processes are weak.
• The domestic market is very small.
• Local manufacturing capabilities remain weak in terms of global competition.
• Progressive approaches to the management of research and higher education
institutions are still insufficient.

 Proposals on the Effective Management of the Technology
Transfer and Science Commercialization System
1.3.1 Education and professional development
• Promote the involvement of persons with decision-making and executive functions
in training and ensure their professional development accordingly.
• Conduct organizational activities (conferences, panels, forums, business trips, etc.)
to support the application of progressive management mechanisms in scientific
research and educational institutions.
• Enable scientific researchers and educators to teach and apply TT and TC skills in
order to ensure academic independence at this level.
• Modernize the educational curricula.

===== PDF_PAGE 250 =====
2022 K-Innovation Partnership Program with Azerbaijan
238
1.3.2 Mentoring services, resource assessment and business networking
• Organize competitions and provid e mentoring support to teams with promising
projects.
• Valuate existing resources and shape success stories with progressive approaches.
• Promote science-education-business cooperation.
• Support global networking.
1.3.3 Innovation capital and its formation
• Organize activities to support the formation of venture capital.
• Provide access to venture capital.
• Organize incubation and acceleration services, including improvement of this field
in Azerbaijan based on Korean experience.
1.3.4 State policy and ecosystem support mechanisms
• Organize lobbying activities in order to achieve effective organization of state policy
in the direction of TT and TC.
• Establish long-term relations with international markets at the state level.
• Share STEPI's experiences in Azerbaijan.
1.3.5 Formation and active operation of the digital platform
• Support the strengthening and popularization of the Technology Transfer and
Science Commercialization Platform (ttcp.az).
• Strengthen ties with global markets by benefiting from Korean experience.
1.3.6 Increasing the relevance of intellectual property rights
• Organize activities to increase the relevance of intellectual property rights.
• Modernize domestic patenting procedures.
• Develop relationships with global patent centers.

2. Strategic Plan for Developing the Technology
Commercialization Program

 Current Situation
In the current era , high technologies form the basis for development of the world
economy. High technology refers to modern technologies used in unique, highly
scientific technological processes, devices and materials, which together determine the
international level of fundamental and applied research. Biotechnologies, laser
techniques and technolog ies, aviation, space research, nuclear energy, genetic
engineering, which allow high technologies to produce competitive products that are
widely used in the priority directions of the country's socio -economic development,
including the improvement of defence capabilities, from the modern scientific-technical
and intellectual potential , computer and micro-opto-electronic technologies, chemistry,
telecommunications and information technologies are related. The intensification of
production and the utilization of the results of scientific and technical progress are
accelerating the rate of innovation of products and services. As mentioned above, the
role of high technologies is decisive in determining the development of the new
economy, its stability and its sustainability.
A techno park is a structural (territorial) or legal entity equipped with advanced
technologies that comprehensively crea tes and adopt s new technologies, produces
modern equipment, devices and facilities and promotes their commercial application.
Techno parks are one of the main tools for the formation of a new type of economy
based on innovation and knowledge.
Applying the successful results of international experience in the creation, organization
and management of innovative technology parks to the structures of the technology
parks being formed in Azerbaijan is considered one of the most urgent issues of the
current period. For Azerbaijan, the main developmental directions consist in developing
innovative entrepreneurship, creating a favourable environment for the development
of new types of activities and products, strengthening the measures for transferring and
adopting advanced technologies, and creating techno parks and innovation zones for
the development and application of science-intensive products and technologies.

===== PDF_PAGE 253 =====
Chapter 5. Strategic Plans for Improving the Technology Commercialization
System and Developing the HTP Program
241
Azerbaijan’s National Academy of Sciences of High Technologies was established by Decree
No. 2425, dated November 8, 2016, of the President of the Republic of Azerbaijan Mr. Ilham
Aliyev. The main goals of creating the HTP are to increase state support for the sustainable
development of the economy, raise competitiveness, expand the innovation and high-tech
areas based on modern scientific and techno logical achievements, conduct scientific
research and create modern complexes for the development of new technologies.

 Main Objectives and Targets of the HTP Program
The main goal of this strategic plan is to create favourable conditions for residents of
Azerbaijan who are engaged in scientific research and practical-constructive work and
commercialize their results in industry, services, and other fields through business and
technological incubators, funds, technological transfer and scientific research centers. The
plan aims to develop the HTP, which ensures their creation and support.
 In order to achieve the main goal, the goals of the HTP according to the development
concept are as follows:
• Development of competitive science-intensive production based on innovative and
high technologies; creation of fertile conditions for service provision , and support
for entrepreneurship in this field.
• Promotion of innovation, scientific research, experimental work, inventions and
discoveries; commercialization of technolog ies and the application thereof to
production.
• Reduction of economic dependence on oil and gas by exploiting scientific and
innovative potential.
• Formation of an environment conducive to scientific and technologically based
ideas that can lead to the development of innovative products.
• Commercialization and promotion of potentially marketable inventions, start -ups
and business ideas developed by residents.

===== PDF_PAGE 254 =====
2022 K-Innovation Partnership Program with Azerbaijan
242
• Raising the competitiveness of science -intensive, high-tech prod ucts in the
international arena.
• Support for the development of the ecosystem in Azerbaijan.
• Development of innovative small businesses and creatio n of new innovation
enterprises.
• Establishment of a favorable environment for the development of small and
medium enterprises in the field of high technology.
• Attraction of science-intensive high technologies and technological products to the
market.
• Transfer of high technolog y; increasing the specific weight of science -intensive
products in the economy.
• Establishment of commercial relations between high -technology developers and
investors.
• Involvement of investments in technological projects at different stages of
development.
• Increase of employment among the working population.
• Formation of relationships with higher education institutions and scientific-research
institutes, including support for the implementation of joint projects.
• Promotion of the scientific research and creative process with the aim of obtaining
innovative scientific knowledge, conducting research, acquiring expertise, and
applying/promoting the results of innovative scientific research.
• Scientific innovation activities, creation of progressive innovations and intellectual
property objects (scientific results, inventions, discoveries, systems, original
technologies, brands, trade and trademarks, etc.) that are formed on the basis of
scientific research ; improvement of the management system and increase d
competitiveness, and support and encourag ement of activities aimed at their
implementation.

 Necessary Conditions for the Development of the HTP
Program
In order to develop competitive science-efficient production based on innovative and high
technologies, and to create fertile conditions for providing services and support
entrepreneurship in this field, the following measures are necessary:
• Obtain greater concessions in the legislation;
• Improve the territory, traffic, management, and social infrastructure of the HTP;
• Construct start-up and business incubators, as well as modern offices for residents'
activities.

To promote innovation, scientific research, experimental work, inventions and discoveries,
and the commercialization and application of technology to HTP production, it is necessary
to create the following conditions:
• Create favorable conditions for the development of innovative projects by large
companies in order to promote rapid industrial assimilation of the results of
scientific research and experimental projects, inventions and discoveries.

To increase the competitiveness of science-intensive high-tech products in the international
arena, the following measures are necessary:
• Gain access to international markets;
• Study the knowledge-based, innovative development experience and problems of
developed countries, and apply that knowledge to the implementation of the HTP
development strategy.

To support the development of the ecosystem in Azerbaijan, the following measures are
necessary:
• Exchange scientific and technological innovative experience with different countries
in order to develop the ecosystem in Azerbaijan.

===== PDF_PAGE 256 =====
2022 K-Innovation Partnership Program with Azerbaijan
244
• Create a favorable environment for the development of small and medium
enterprises in the field of high technology.
• Support the creation and development of small and medium -sized innovative
enterprises that are residents of the techno park.

To attract investments to technological projects in various stages of development, the
following measures are necessary:
• Establish a favorable business investment environment.
• Coordinate the implementation of innovative projects, develop test -expertise and
prototypes, and commercialize science activities with international cooperation and
grant programs, including the involvement of potential donors.
• Create favorable conditions for financing innovation projects, attract and promote
investments, and carry out the relevant organizational work.

To support the transfer of high technology from its developers to various sectors of industry,
and to develop partnerships between the public and private sectors of the economy, the
following measures are necessary:
• Establish a mechanism for issuing state orders concerning products produced in the
HTP.
• Promote c ooperation with higher education institutions and scientific research
institutes in order to support the implementation of joint projects, as well as
fostering relations with higher education institutions and scientific research
institutes.

In order to promote the scientific research and creative process with the aim of obtaining
innovative scientific knowledge, conducting research, acquiring expertise, and
applying/promoting the results of innovative scientific research, the following measures are
necessary:
• Support the implementation of scientific and technical projects and programs
aimed at creating science-metric technologies and competitive products.

===== PDF_PAGE 257 =====

• Support scientific research works of a high level and great practical importance, and
which can contribute to the economic development of the country and lead to
important progress through the application of new technologies.

The non-existence of the conditions listed above will either make it impossible to achieve
the main goals and objectives, or lead to poor or ineffective results.

 Necessary Steps for the Development of the HTP Program
① Coordinate university-industry cooperation ; create opportunities for the
application of science, including intellectual property issues ; provide mentoring
services to technological entrepreneurs, and ensure that research conducted in
academia and universities receives more support from financial sources; protect
and commercializ e knowledge obtained in academia ; establish a Technology
Transfer Office staffed by professionals to support academic entrepreneurship.
② Create favorable conditions for attracting capital and other in vestments for
venture financing.
③ Take necessary measures to optimize tax rates, mandatory state social insurance
fees and customs duties.
④ Take necessary measures to simplify the certification and licensing procedure:
Simplify the certification and licensing procedure of high-tech products and equipment
produced by HTP residents, as well as products and equipment necessary for their
production and imported from abroad, as this will have a positive effect on the
dynamics of production acceleration in this area.
⑤ Offer favorable rental prices to residents (by HTTP):
In order to minimize the expenses of residents engaged in entrepreneurial
activities and stimulate the production of science-efficient technological products,
favorable rental prices will be offered by the HTP.
⑥ Apply extraterritorial residency in the HTP:

===== PDF_PAGE 258 =====
2022 K-Innovation Partnership Program with Azerbaijan
246
Operating the HTP according to the extraterritorial principle should be one of the
main priority directions. Legal and natural persons to be registered in the HTP may
use all relevant benefits regardless of the location of their place of business.
⑦ Develop business incubators and start-up projects, and create model enterprises:
The state budget, donations from donor organizations, as well as funds from local
and foreign investors should be used to improve the operation of the existing
business incubator in the HTP, and to create new incubators. The development of
appropriate financing mechanisms should be considered to ensure access to
initial financial resources for other start -ups and those who wish to establish a
new business participating in the HTP business incubat or. Start-up projects can
serve as a platform for entrepreneur s who want to start a business or execute
their new ideas. Steps related to ensuring the participation of the private sector,
donor organizations and various financial institutions in the financing of start -up
projects should be determined and various projects should be reviewed.
⑧ Create innovation funds - legal entities with non-commercial status that carry out
targeted financing of scientific research in the form of grants :
Innovation fund s make it possible to increase the quality and efficiency of
scientific research in the HTP, and to provide targeted financing for applied and
innovative scientific programs and projects. The development of innovative
infrastructure and the movement of local innovations to international markets
will be ensured ; and the transfer, exchange and devel opment of innovative
technologies, as well as their commercialization and application, will be realized.
⑨ Create and develop an information portfolio on innovative research works in
higher education institutions and scientific research institutes, as well as the
involvement of scientific applied works in the HTP.
⑩ Improve the territory of the HTP ; restore serviceable buildings and facilities for
adaptation to new projects; demolish non-serviceable buildings and facilities and
construct new buildings in their place; construct new roads and restor e existing
ones. To improve the social welfare of re sidents' employees and families, create

Chapter 5. Strategic Plans for Improving the Technology Commercialization
System and Developing the HTP Program
247
a complex of residential, social, healthcare, educational, cultural, catering, sports,
entertainment and other facilities within the HTP.
⑪ Sale of products produced in the HTP on the basis of state orders:
This results in the improvement and maintenance of the infrastructure of the HTP,
including the development of start-ups, business incubation, and the technology
transfer and commercialization processes in accordance with its mission, along
with internal sources, innovation or venture funds, budget funds allocated by the
state, including direct subsidies provided by the state.

 Concept of Incubation and the Technology Transfer Centre
of the HTP
2.5.1 Main purposes of the concept
• To assist in the commercialization of scientific research activities carried out in
scientific institutions.
• To create economic, social and cultural values as a result of this process.
• To establish cooperation between universities and private sector organizations.
• To increase existing cooperation in order to produce the information an d
technology needed by industry.
• To coordinate technology transfer and the development of concrete results
between universities and industry.

Reestablishment of the center is expected to contribute to the following areas: research,
the development of and support for research and development projects, the registration
and protection of intellectual and industrial property rights, marketing, commercialization,
support for venture capital, the establishment of an incubation center, business
management, and consulting and training services.

===== PDF_PAGE 260 =====
2022 K-Innovation Partnership Program with Azerbaijan
248
2.5.2 Vision
• To bring research innovations to the market with an effective technolo gy transfer
strategy, develop high value -added projects between universitie s and industry,
actively pursu e academic entrepre neurship research, and expand
internationalization activities.
• To create a world -renowned, effective, enterprising, advanced and sustainable
technology transfer office with a rich technology portfolio, and to realize successful
commercialization activities and strong collaboration between universities and
industry.

2.5.3 Responsibilities
① Develop and implemen t marketing and commercializ ation strategies necessary
for transferring the portfolio of intellectual and industrial rights to industry,
including patents, utility models, and industrial designs belonging to the Academy;
② Conduct preliminary research on the patentability and commercialization of
reported inventions.
③ Manage the relationships, negotiations and contract ual processes with potential
buyers and investors for the licensing or transfer of intellectual and industrial
property rights with commercialization potential.
④ Expand the national and international cooperation network of the Incubation and
Technology Transfer Center of ANAS HTP and organize events aimed at increasing
cooperation, potential, and promotion.
⑤ Represent the Incubation and Technology Transfer Center of the HTP of ANAS in
front of companies, investors, government agencies and other scientific and
educational institutions in the country and overseas.
⑥ Carry out project writing and project management training.

2.6.4 Intellectual and industrial rights management and licensing services

2.6.5 Corporate and entrepreneurial services

• Provide fixed capital, incubation and business management services to mediate the
creation of spin-off companies.

2022 K-Innovation Partnership Program with Azerbaijan

===== PDF_PAGE 265 =====
Chapter 6. Conclusion
253

1. Conclusion

The 2022 K-Innovation Partnership Program aims to strengthen the operation and potential
competitiveness of the HTP through the Technology Commercialization Knowledge Lab
(TCK-Lab) and training program, and to support the formation and improvement of an
innovative economic ecosystem in Azerbaijan.
Firstly, TCK-Lab has provided training for the members of the beneficiary group of
Azerbaijan through seven online lectures on the commercialization of tec hnologies,
intellectual properties, start-up and incubation, and university-industry cooperation, which
were presented in the format of a "knowledge laboratory" by Korean experts.
The 3-day training workshop was carried out online due to the COVID-19 situation. In the
workshop, Korean experts from universities, PRIs, and enterprises delivered lectures to
twenty Azerbaijan experts with the aim of equipping them with more practical knowledge.
In particular, the Korean experts shared their experience in areas requiring improvement in
Azerbaijan, such as industry-academia-research cooperation platforms and technology tech
valuation.
The “dissemination forum” of the 2022 project was held with the support of Azerbaijan's
Ministry of Science and Education. This forum introduced STEPI and the K -Innovation
Partnership Program, and served to inform technology commercialization stakeholders
about the importance of the HTP's role in improving Azerbaijan's technology
commercialization system and creating an ecosystem.

Chapter 6. Conclusion

===== PDF_PAGE 266 =====
2022 K-Innovation Partnership Program with Azerbaijan
254
Lastly, theories on, and Korea’s experience of, technology commercialization governance,
intellectual property management, and start-up programs contributed decisively to
establishing strategic plans for improving the technology commercialization system in
Azerbaijan and the HTP program development plans.
Concerning the activities of the project for the year 2022, the following expectations have
been defined:
• Capacity building of the stakeholders in the Azerbaijan innovation ecosystem.
• Contribution to the formation of human capital in Azerbaijan.
• Expansion of Azerbaijan-Korea scientific, technology and innovation (STI)
cooperation.
• Improvement of the operation program of the HTP.
• Further improve ment of the process of technology commercialization should be
observed with the transformation of knowledge into economic value.
• Formation of a new entrepre neurial environment based on Korea’s experience of
start-up and business incubation

We hope that the training and other methods of skills development carried out by well-
known experts of the Republic of Korea and local consultants will have a significant and
meaningful impact on the development of representatives of the High Technology Park and
other organizations represented in the beneficiary group.
